Existence and convergence of the Beris-Edwards system with general Landau-de Gennes energy
Zhewen Feng, Min-Chun Hong and Yu Mei

Abstract
. In this paper, we investigate the Beris-Edwards system for both biaxial and uniaxial Q Q -tensors with a general Landau-de Gennes energy density depending on four non-zero elastic constants. We prove existence of the strong solution of the Beris-Edwards system for uniaxial Q Q -tensors up to a maximal time. Furthermore, we prove that the strong solutions of the Beris-Edwards system for biaxial Q Q -tensors converge smoothly to the solution of the Beris-Edwards system for uniaxial Q Q -tensors up to its maximal existence time.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
35K51,35Q30,76A15

1.
Introduction

The classical Ericksen-Leslie
theory (
[
9
]
,
[
19
]
) successfully describes the dynamic
flow of uniaxial nematic liquid crystals. In
[
3
]
, Beris-Edwards pointed out that the Ericksen-Leslie
flow theory has a limited domain of applications to liquid crystals. Therefore, based on the celebrated Landau-de Gennes
Q
-tensor theory,
Beris-Edwards
[
3
]
proposed a general hydrodynamic theory to describe flows of liquid crystals in modeling both uniaxial and biaxial nematic liquid crystals.

In 1971, de Gennes
[
6
]
introduced a
Q
-tensor order parameter to establish the Landau-de Gennes theory, which has been one of the successful continuum theories in modeling both uniaxial and biaxial nematic liquid crystals (c.f.
[
7
]
,
[
1
]
). Mathematically, the Landau-de Gennes theory is described by a Landau-de Gennes functional in
the space of symmetric and traceless
3\times 3
matrices

S_{0}:=\left\{Q\in\mathbb{M}^{3\times 3}:\quad Q^{T}=Q,\,\mbox{tr }Q=0\right\},

where
\mathbb{M}^{3\times 3}
denotes the space of
3\times 3
matrices. Let
S_{*}
be the space of all uniaxial
Q
-tensors defined by

S_{*}:=\left\{Q\in S_{0}:\quad Q=s_{+}(u\otimes u-\frac{1}{3}I),\quad u\in S^{2},\quad s_{+}:=\frac{b+\sqrt{b^{2}+24ac}}{4c}\right\},

where the constants
a
,
b
,
c
correspond to a lower temperature regime in liquid crystals and we assume that
a
,
b
,
c
are positive.
Let
U
be a domain in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. For a tensor
Q\in W^{1,2}(U;S_{0})
,
the original Landau-de Gennes energy is defined by

E_{LG}(Q;U):=\int_{U}f_{LG}\,dx=\int_{U}(\tilde{f}_{E}+\tilde{f}_{B})\,dx.

(1.1)

Here
\tilde{f}_{E}
is the elastic energy density with elastic constants
L_{1},...,L_{4}
of the form

\tilde{f}_{E}(Q,\nabla Q):=\frac{L_{1}}{2}|\nabla Q|^{2}+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\frac{\partial Q_{ij}}{\partial x_{j}}\frac{\partial Q_{ik}}{\partial x_{k}}+\frac{L_{3}}{2}\frac{\partial Q_{ik}}{\partial x_{j}}\frac{\partial Q_{ij}}{\partial x_{k}}+\frac{L_{4}}{2}Q_{lk}\frac{\partial Q_{ij}}{\partial x_{l}}\frac{\partial Q_{ij}}{\partial x_{k}}

(1.2)

in which and in the sequel, we adopt the Einstein summation convention for repeated indices and
\tilde{f}_{B}(Q)
is a bulk energy density defined by

\tilde{f}_{B}(Q):=-\frac{a}{2}\tr(Q^{2})-\frac{b}{3}\tr(Q^{3})+\frac{c}{4}\left[\tr(Q^{2})\right]^{2}

(1.3)

with three positive material constants
a,b,c
.

In
[
6
]
, de Gennes discovered first two terms of the elastic energy density in (
1.2
) with
L_{3}=L_{4}=0
.
Later, combining the work of Schiele-Trimper
[
30
]
with the effect of Berreman-Meiboom
[
4
]
, Dickmann
[
8
]
completed the full density (
1.2
) with two additional terms (c.f.
[
22
]
,
[
1
]
) in which the elastic energy density is consistent with
the Oseen-Frank density in for uniaxial nematic liquid crystals. However, for the case of
L_{4}\neq 0
, Ball-Majumdar
[
2
]
found an example that the Landau-de Gennes energy density (
1.2
) does not satisfy the coercivity condition. In fact, Golovaty et al.
[
13
]
said that “From the standpoint of energy minimization, unfortunately, such a version of Landau-de Gennes becomes problematic, since the inclusion of the cubic term leads to an energy which is unbounded from below”. Therefore, there is a problem between mathematical and physical theory on nematic liquid crystals in the case of
L_{4}\neq 0
. In their book
[
7
]
, de Gennes and Prost said that
“the bending constant is much larger than others”
; i.e.
k_{3}>\max\{{k_{1},k_{2}\}}
at different temperatures. For example, for p-azoxyanisole (PAA) at
134^{\circ}C
,
k_{1}=4.05
,
k_{2}=2.1
,
k_{3}=5.77
,
k_{4}=3.08
(see
[
30
]
). By the physical experiments on liquid crystals, the elastic constant
L_{4}=\frac{1}{2s_{+}^{3}}(k_{3}-k_{1})
is not zero in general.

To solve the above coercivity problem on the Landau-de Gennes energy density in the case of
L_{4}\neq 0
, it was observed in
[
10
]
that for uniaxial tensors,
the original
third order term on
L_{4}
in (
1.2
), proposed by
Schiele and Trimper
[
30
]
*p. 268 in physics, is a linear combination of a fourth order term and a second order term; i.e. for
Q\in S_{*}
, we have

\displaystyle Q_{lk}\frac{\partial Q_{ij}}{\partial x_{l}}\frac{\partial Q_{ij}}{\partial x_{k}}=\frac{3}{s_{+}}(Q_{ln}\frac{\partial Q_{ij}}{\partial x_{l}})(Q_{kn}\frac{\partial Q_{ij}}{\partial x_{k}})-\frac{2s_{+}}{3}|\nabla Q|^{2}.

(1.4)

Therefore, Feng and Hong

[
10
]
introduced
a new Landau-de Gennes energy given by

E_{LG}(Q;U):=\int_{U}f(Q,\nabla Q)\,dx=\int_{U}\left(f_{E}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}f_{B}(Q)\right)\,dx,

(1.5)

where

\displaystyle f_{E}(Q,\nabla Q)=

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{L}_{1}}{2}|\nabla Q|^{2}+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\frac{\partial Q_{ij}}{\partial x_{j}}\frac{\partial Q_{ik}}{\partial x_{k}}+\frac{L_{3}}{2}\frac{\partial Q_{ik}}{\partial x_{j}}\frac{\partial Q_{ij}}{\partial x_{k}}+\frac{3}{2s_{+}}L^{(4)}(Q,\nabla Q)

(1.6)

with
\tilde{L}_{1}=L_{1}-\frac{2}{3s_{+}}L_{4}
for
L_{4}\geq 0
,
\tilde{L}_{1}=L_{1}+\frac{4}{3s_{+}}L_{4}
for
L_{4}<0
,

\displaystyle L^{(4)}(Q,\nabla Q):=\begin{cases}L_{4}Q_{ln}Q_{kn}\frac{\partial Q_{ij}}{\partial x_{l}}\frac{\partial Q_{ij}}{\partial x_{k}}&\mbox{ for }L_{4}\geq 0,\\
L_{4}[Q_{ln}Q_{kn}\frac{\partial Q_{ij}}{\partial x_{l}}\frac{\partial Q_{ij}}{\partial x_{k}}-|Q|^{2}|\nabla Q|^{2}]&\mbox{ for }L_{4}<0\end{cases}

and

f_{B}(Q):=\tilde{f}_{B}(Q)-\min_{Q\in S_{0}}\tilde{f}_{B}(Q)\geq 0.

The new elastic energy density (
1.6
) keeps three physical terms of the original Landau-de Gennes density (
1.2
) and is equivalent to the original density (
1.2
) for
Q\in S_{*}
. In (
1.5
), the constant
L
is a rescaled dimensionless parameter, which drives four elastic constants

\tilde{L}_{1},\cdots,L_{4}
to zero simultaneously as
L
tends to zero. This corresponds to the large body limit which is of great importance in physics (c.f.
[
1
]
,
[
12
]
).
We always assume that the constants
\tilde{L}_{1},L_{2}
and
L_{3}
satisfy

\displaystyle\tilde{L}_{1}+L_{3}>0,\quad 2\tilde{L}_{1}-L_{3}>0,\quad\tilde{L}_{1}+\frac{5}{3}L_{2}+\frac{1}{6}L_{3}>0.

(1.7)

Under the condition (
1.7
), the new Landau-de Gennes elastic energy density in (
1.5
) satisfies the coercivity condition; i.e.
f(Q,\nabla Q)
for any
Q\in S_{0}
is bounded from below by
\frac{\alpha}{2}|\nabla Q|^{2}
with some
\alpha>0
(c.f.
[
18
]
,
[
10
]
).

In this paper, we investigate the Beris-Edwards system for the Landau-de Gennes energy (
1.5
) with
L_{4}\neq 0
. The Beris-Edwards system with
L_{2}=L_{3}=L_{4}=0
has been extensively studied by many authors (see
[
27
]
,
[
28
]
,
[
33
]
). The Beris-Edwards system introduced in
[
3
]
is a system of coupling Navier-Stokes equations with the gradient flow for the Landau-de Gennes energy. More precisely, let
v:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
be the velocity of the fluid and let
Q:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to S_{0}
be a
Q
-tensor order parameter, which depends on the director of the molecular field. The symmetric and skew-symmetric parts of the tensor
\nabla v
are

D=\frac{1}{2}(\nabla v+(\nabla v)^{T}),\quad\Omega=\frac{1}{2}(\nabla v-(\nabla v)^{T}).

Define
[Q,\Omega]:=Q\Omega-\Omega Q
to be the Lie bracket product and set

S(Q,v)=\xi\Big(D(Q+\frac{1}{3}I)+(Q+\frac{1}{3}I)D-2(Q+\frac{1}{3}I)(Q\cdot D)\Big)-[Q,\Omega].

Then the Beris-Edwards system (c.f.
[
3
]
,
[
28
]
) is given by

\displaystyle\partial_{t}v+v\cdot\nabla v-\nu\Delta v+\nabla P=

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\Big(\tau(Q,\nabla Q)+\sigma(Q,\nabla Q)\Big),

(1.8)

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot v=

\displaystyle 0,

(1.9)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Q+v\cdot\nabla Q-S(Q,v)=

\displaystyle\Gamma H(Q,\nabla Q),

(1.10)

where
H(Q,\nabla Q)
is the molecular field,
P
is the pressure, the antisymmetric part of the distortion stress
\tau(Q,\nabla Q)=[Q,H]
and
\sigma(Q,\nabla Q)
is the distortion stress (c.f.
[
29
]
) given by

\sigma_{ij}(Q,\nabla Q)=-\xi(QH+HQ+\frac{2}{3}H)_{ij}+2\xi(Q\cdot H)(Q+\frac{I}{3})_{ij}-\partial_{p_{kl}^{j}}f(Q,\nabla Q)\nabla_{i}Q_{kl}.

Here and in the sequel, we denote
\partial_{p_{kl}^{j}}f(Q,\nabla Q):=\frac{\partial f(Q,\nabla Q)}{\partial(\nabla_{j}Q_{kl})}
with
p=\nabla Q
.

For simplicity, we assume that
\xi=0,\,\Gamma=\nu=1
in the Beris-Edwards system.
For biaxial
Q
-tensors, we use the Landau-de Gennes energy
density (
1.5
) to formulate the Beris-Edwards system with
L_{4}\neq 0
. The rescaled Beris-Edwards system for
Q_{L}\in S_{0}
and
v_{L}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
is:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}v_{L}+v_{L}\cdot\nabla v_{L}-\Delta v_{L}+\nabla P_{L}=

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\Big([Q_{L},\mathcal{H}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})]+\sigma(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})\Big),

(1.11)

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot v_{L}=

\displaystyle 0,

(1.12)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Q_{L}+v_{L}\cdot\nabla Q_{L}+[Q_{L},\Omega_{L}]=

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q_{L}).

(1.13)

The molecular field
H_{L}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})
is then given by

H_{L}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})=:\mathcal{H}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q_{L}),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})_{ij}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\nabla_{k}[\partial_{p_{ij}^{k}}f_{E}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})]+\nabla_{k}[\partial_{p_{ji}^{k}}f_{E}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})]\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{Q_{ij}}f_{E}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})+\partial_{Q_{ji}}f_{E}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\sum_{l=1}^{3}\left(\nabla_{k}[\partial_{p_{ll}^{k}}f_{E}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})]-\partial_{Q_{ll}}f_{E}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})\right),

(1.14)

the term
g_{B}(Q_{L})
is

\displaystyle g_{B}(Q_{L}):=

\displaystyle aQ_{L}+b\big(Q_{L}Q_{L}-\frac{1}{3}\tr(Q_{L}^{2})I\big)-cQ_{L}\tr(Q_{L}^{2})

(1.15)

and
\sigma(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})
is the distortion stress tensor with

\nabla_{j}\sigma_{ij}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})=-\nabla_{j}\left(\nabla_{i}(Q_{L})_{kl}\partial_{p_{kl}^{j}}f_{E}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})\right).

Set

H^{2}_{Q_{e}}(\mathbb{R}^{3};S_{0})=\{Q\in S_{0}:Q-Q_{e}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\},

where
Q_{e}=s_{+}(e\otimes e-\frac{1}{3}I)\in S_{*}
and
e\in S^{2}
is a constant vector.

We call
(Q_{L},v_{L})
to be a strong solution to the system (
1.11
)-(
1.13
) in
\mathbb{R}^{3}\times(0,T)
for some
T>0
if it satisfies the system a.e. in
(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times(0,T)
and

\displaystyle Q_{L}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{3}_{Q_{e}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))\cap L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}_{Q_{e}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})),\quad\partial_{t}Q_{L}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})),

\displaystyle v_{L}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))\cap L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})).

Then we have

Theorem 1

(Local Existence)
.

Let
(Q_{L,0},v_{L,0})\in H^{2}_{Q_{e}}(\mathbb{R}^{3};S_{0})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3};\mathbb{R}^{3})
be the initial values satisfying
\div v_{L,0}=0

and
\|Q_{L,0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq K
for a constant
K>0
.
Then there is a unique strong
solution
(Q_{L},v_{L})
to the system (
1.11
)-(
1.13
) in
\mathbb{R}^{3}\times[0,T)
with initial data
(Q_{L,0},v_{L,0})
for some

T>0
.

Although Theorem
1
might be known for some experts, see
[
28
]
for the case
L_{2}=L_{3}=L_{4}=0
.
However, since there exists some new difficulty on
f_{E}
with
L_{4}\neq 0
, we give a detailed proof in Section 5 for completeness.

Next, we will formulate the Beris-Edwards system for uniaxial Q-tensors.
In their book
[
3
]
, Beris-Edwards suggested the hydrodynamic theory to describe flows of liquid
crystals for uniaxial Q-tensors
Q\in S_{*}
, but they could not write an explicit form of molecular field
H(Q,\nabla Q)
for
Q\in S_{*}

with nonzero elastic constants
L_{2}
,
L_{3}
,
L_{4}
. Recently, the explicit form of the molecular field
H(Q,\nabla Q)
for
Q\in S_{*}
with general elastic constants was given in
[
10
]
, so we can apply the form to formulate the Beris-Edwards system for uniaxial Q-tensors.
For any two matrix
A,B\in S_{0}
, we denote the standard product by
\left\langle A,B\right\rangle:=\sum_{i,j}A_{ij}B_{ij}
.
Then, the molecular field for
Q\in S_{*}
is

\displaystyle H(Q,\nabla Q)=\nabla_{k}\left(\partial_{p^{k}}f_{E}(Q+\frac{s_{+}}{3}I)+(Q+\frac{s_{+}}{3}I)(\partial_{p^{k}}f_{E})^{T}\right)

\displaystyle-2s_{+}^{-1}\nabla_{k}\left((Q+\frac{s_{+}}{3}I)\left\langle Q+\frac{s_{+}}{3}I,\,\partial_{p^{k}}f_{E}\right\rangle\right)-\partial_{p^{k}}f_{E}\nabla_{k}Q-\nabla_{k}Q(\partial_{p^{k}}f_{E})^{T}

\displaystyle+2s_{+}^{-1}\left[\left\langle\partial_{p^{k}}f_{E},\nabla_{k}Q\right\rangle(Q+\frac{s_{+}}{3}I)+\left\langle\partial_{p^{k}}f_{E},(Q+\frac{s_{+}}{3}I)\right\rangle\nabla_{k}Q\right]

\displaystyle-\partial_{Q}f_{E}(Q+\frac{s_{+}}{3}I)-(Q+\frac{s_{+}}{3}I)(\partial_{Q}f_{E})^{T}+2s_{+}^{-1}\left\langle\partial_{Q}f_{E},Q+\frac{s_{+}}{3}I\right\rangle(Q+\frac{s_{+}}{3}I).

(1.16)

Through the new molecular field (
1.16
) with a uniaxial Q-tensors
Q\in S_{*}
, the Beris-Edwards system for nonzero elastic constants
L_{1},\cdots,L_{4}
is:

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla-\Delta)v+\nabla P=

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\Big([Q,H]+\sigma(Q,\nabla Q)\Big),

(1.17)

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot v=

\displaystyle 0,

(1.18)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla)Q+[Q,\Omega]=

\displaystyle H(Q,\nabla Q).

(1.19)

Then we prove the existence of strong solutions to (
1.17
)-(
1.19
) in the following:

Theorem 2
.

Assume that
(Q_{0},v_{0})\in H^{2}_{Q_{e}}(\mathbb{R}^{3};S_{*})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3};\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
\div v_{0}=0
. Then there is a unique strong
solution
(Q,v)
to the system (
1.17
)-(
1.19
) in
\mathbb{R}^{3}\times[0,T^{*})
with initial data
(Q_{0},v_{0})
.
Moreover, there are two positive constants
\varepsilon_{0}
and
R_{0}
such that at a singular point
x_{i}
, the maximal existence time
T^{*}
satisfies

\operatornamewithlimits{lim\,sup}_{t\rightarrow T^{*}}\int_{B_{R}(x_{i})}|\nabla Q(x,t)|^{3}+|v(x,t)|^{3}\,dx\geq\varepsilon_{0}^{3}

for any
R>0
with
R\leq R_{0}
.

For the proof of Theorem
2
, one of the key steps is to establish Proposition
3.1
and obtain that for a short time
T_{1}>0
, the strong solution to the system (
1.11
)-(
1.13
) with initial data
(Q_{0},v_{0})
satisfies the uniform estimate:

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq s\leq T_{1}}\left(\|\nabla Q_{L}(s)\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}+\|v_{L}(s)\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}+\frac{1}{L}\|Q_{L}(s)-\pi(Q_{L}(s))\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\|\nabla^{2}Q_{L}\|_{L^{2}(0,T_{1};H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}Q_{L}\|_{L^{2}(0,T_{1};H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\nabla v_{L}\|_{L^{2}(0,T_{1};H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}^{2}+\frac{1}{L}\|\nabla(Q_{L}-\pi(Q_{L}))\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\leq C.

Here
\pi(Q_{L})
is the projection of
Q_{L}
defined below in the proof of Theorem
3
.
The proof of Proposition
3.1
is sophisticated and it will also play a crucial role in the proof of Theorem
3
below. We will outline more details about it later.

Remark 1
.

It was pointed out in
[
3
]
that (
1.17
)-(
1.19
) can be reduced to the hydrodynamic flow of the Oseen-Franks energy, known as the Ericksen-Leslie system. In fact, multiplying
u_{j}
to (
1.19
) and employing
|u|^{2}=1
, one can check that

\sigma(Q,\nabla Q)=-\nabla u^{T}\frac{\partial W(u,\nabla u)}{\partial(\nabla u)},\quad\partial_{p_{kl}^{j}}f_{E}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})=s_{+}^{-1}u_{l}\frac{\partial W(u,\nabla u)}{\partial(\nabla_{j}u_{k})}.

Ericksen
[
9
]
and Leslie
[
19
]
in the 1960s proposed the celebrated hydrodynamic theory to describe the behavior of liquid crystal flows.
The question on the
global existence of weak solutions to the Ericksen-Leslie system is very challenging.
Similarly to the result of Chen and Struwe
[
5
]
on the heat flow for harmonic maps, Lin and Liu
[
21
]
introduced the Ginzburg-Landau approximation for the Ericksen-Leslie system to solve the existence problem, but they could not show that the solutions of the Ginzburg-Landau approximate systems approach the solution of the Ericksen-Leslie system.
In
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, Hong
[
14
]
and Hong-Xin
[
17
]
proved that the solutions of the Ginzburg-Landau approximate systems with unequal Frank constants approach the solution of the Ericksen-Leslie system in a short time and showed the global existence of weak solutions to the Ericksen-Leslie system in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
by using the idea of Struwe
[
32
]
on the harmonic map flow.
In
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, Hong, Li and Xin
[
15
]
showed the strong convergence of the Ginzburg-Landau approximate system with unequal Frank constants before the blow-up time of the Ericksen-Leslie system. Recently, we
[
11
]
improved the result in
[
15
]
by proving the smooth convergence of the Ginzburg-Landau approximate systems for a general Ericksen-Leslie system with Leslie tensors before the blow-up time.

By comparing with the convergence of
Ginzburg-Landau models for superconductivity theory, Gartland
[
12
]
emphasised importance of the convergence on Landau-de Gennes solutions. In physics, both the Ericksen-Leslie
theory and the Beris-Edwards theory should unify in modelling uniaxial state of nematic liquid crystals, so it is very
interesting to give a mathematical proof to verify that the solutions of the Beris-Edwards system (
1.11
)-(
1.13
)
can approach a solution of the Ericksen-Leslie
Q
-tensor system as
L\to 0
.
We solve the convergence problem of the Beris-Edwards system in the following:

Theorem 3
.

Assume that
(Q_{0},v_{0})\in H_{Q_{e}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3};S_{*})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3};\mathbb{R}^{3})
with
\div v_{0}=0
.
For each
L>0
, let
(Q_{L},v_{L})
be the unique strong solution to the system (
1.11
)-(
1.13
) in

\mathbb{R}^{3}\times[0,T_{L})
with initial data
(Q_{0},v_{0})
for the maximal existence time
T_{L}
.
Let
(Q,v)
be the strong solution to the system (
1.17
)-(
1.19
) in
\mathbb{R}^{3}\times[0,T^{*})
with the same initial data
(Q_{0},v_{0})
and the maximal existence time
T^{*}
in Theorem
2
. Then, for any
T\in(0,T^{*})
, there exists a sufficiently small
L_{T}>0
such that
T\leq T_{L}
for any
L\leq L_{T}
.
Moreover, as
L\to 0
, we have

(\nabla Q_{L},v_{L})\rightarrow(\nabla Q,v)\qquad\text{in }~~L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))

(1.20)

and

(\nabla Q_{L},v_{L})\rightarrow(\nabla Q,v)\qquad\text{in }~~C^{\infty}(\tau,T;C_{loc}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))\quad\text{for any }\tau>0.

(1.21)

For the proof of Theorem
3
, the main ideas are to establish uniform estimates on higher order derivatives of
(Q_{L},v_{L})
in
L
. Using similar methods in
[
17
]
,
[
11
]
, we can handle all terms involving
f_{E}(Q_{L})
, but the main difficulty is to obtain the uniform estimate of the terms involving
\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q_{L})
when
L\to 0
. To handle those difficult terms, we use a concept of a projection near
S_{*}
, which was first introduced on Riemannian manifolds by Schoen and Uhlenbeck
[
31
]
. Denote

\displaystyle S_{\delta}:=\left\{Q\in S_{0}:\quad\dist(Q;S_{*})\leq\delta\right\}.

(1.22)

Let
\pi:S_{\delta}\to S_{*}
be the smooth projection map for a small
\delta>0
so that
\pi(Q)
is the nearest point; i.e.
|Q-\pi(Q)|=\dist(Q;S_{*})
for

Q\in S_{\delta}
.
For each smooth
Q_{L}(x)\in S_{\delta}
, there is a rotation
R(Q_{L}(x))\in SO(3)
such that
R^{T}(Q_{L}(x))Q_{L}(x)R(Q_{L}(x))
is diagonal. However,
R(Q_{L}(x))
is not always smooth; i.e there exists a measure zero set
\Sigma_{L}
such that
R(Q_{L}(x))
is differentiable in
U
except for the singular set
\Sigma_{L}
. To study the convergence of solutions of the Landau-de Gennes system, Feng-Hong
[
10
]
proved a geometric identity
\nabla\left(R^{T}(Q)QR(Q)\right)_{ii}=(R^{T}(Q)\nabla QR(Q))_{ii}
with
i=1,2,3
away from the singular set for handling the difficulty on
\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q_{L})
. Due to the singular set
\Sigma_{L}
of the rotation
R(Q_{L}(x))\in SO(3)
, the proof in
[
10
]
is much complicated. In this paper,
we find a new approach to avoid the main difficulty arising from the singular set
\Sigma_{L}
of the above rotation
R(Q_{L}(x))
in
[
10
]
. We outline main steps of the new approach as follows:

The first key step is to establish some new estimates to overcome the difficulty arising from the term
f_{B}(Q_{L})
.
For each smooth
Q\in S_{\delta}
,
\pi(Q)\in S_{*}
has a constant number of distinct eigenvalues, so there exists a smooth rotation
R_{Q}:=R(\pi(Q))\in SO(3)
such that

\displaystyle R^{T}_{Q}\pi(Q)R_{Q}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{-s_{+}}{3}&0&0\\
0&\frac{-s_{+}}{3}&0\\
0&0&\frac{2s_{+}}{3}\end{pmatrix}=:Q^{+}.

(1.23)

Since
\pi(Q)
commutes with
Q
for any
Q\in S_{\delta}
(c.f.
[
25
]
), one can see

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}=R^{T}_{Q}QR_{Q}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{Q}_{11}&\tilde{Q}_{12}&0\\
\tilde{Q}_{21}&\tilde{Q}_{22}&0\\
0&0&\tilde{Q}_{33}\end{pmatrix}.

(1.24)

For any
Q\in S_{\delta}
, we can derive an estimate

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{2}|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}\leq\partial^{2}_{\tilde{Q}_{ij}\tilde{Q}_{kl}}f_{B}(\tilde{Q})\nabla_{x_{\beta}}\tilde{Q}_{ij}\nabla_{x_{\beta}}\tilde{Q}_{kl}+C|\nabla Q|^{2}|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}

(1.25)

with some constant
\lambda>0
, which improves a result of diagonal matrices in
[
10
]
.

The second key step is to establish the uniform estimate on
(\nabla^{2}Q_{L},\nabla v_{L})
. To overcome the difficulty arising from the term
g_{B}(Q_{L})
,
we rotate the equation (
1.13
) by
R_{Q_{L}}\in SO(3)
such that
g_{B}(\tilde{Q}_{L})
has the same matrix form of
\tilde{Q}
in (
1.24
). For any
Q\in S_{\delta}
, we find an extension of the geometric identity of
\tilde{Q}
in (
1.24
) that

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla g_{B}(\tilde{Q}),\nabla R_{Q}^{T}\pi(Q)R_{Q}-R_{Q}^{T}\pi(Q)\nabla R_{Q}\right\rangle=0.

(1.26)

Combining the above identity (
1.25
) with (
1.26
), we establish the uniform estimate in Lemmas
2.5
-
2.6
.
By a local
L^{3}
-type of estimate

\displaystyle\sup_{T_{0}\leq t\leq T_{L},x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{B_{R}(x_{0})}|\nabla Q_{L}|^{3}+|v_{L}|^{3}+\frac{|Q_{L}-\pi(Q_{L})|^{3}}{L^{\frac{3}{2}}}\,dx\leq\varepsilon_{0}^{3},

(1.27)

we establish a key Proposition
3.1
and then prove Theorem
2
, which is similar to the idea in
[
11
]
. Finally, by an induction method, we establish the sophisticated uniform estimate of
(\nabla^{k+1}Q_{L},\nabla^{k}v_{L})
in
L
for any integer
k\geq 2
to prove Theorem
3
. We would point out that our proof on high order uniform estimates is new and different from one used for Ginzburg-Landau approximations in
[
11
]
.

Remark 2
.

When
L_{4}=0
, Wang-Zhang-Zhang
[
34
]
proved some related convergence of (
1.11
)-(
1.13
) with smooth initial values
to the Ericksen-Leslie system in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, but not to
the uniaxial Q-tensor Beris-Edwards system (
1.17
)-(
1.19
). It seems that their method only works for smooth initial values.
Recently, Xin-Zhang
[
35
]
proved that the weak convergence also holds in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
for (
1.11
)-(
1.13
) with
L_{2}=L_{3}=L_{4}=0
.

The paper is organized as follows. In Section 2, we derive some a-priori estimates on the strong solution
(Q_{L},v_{L})
of the system (
1.11
)-(
1.13
) in

\mathbb{R}^{3}\times[0,T_{L}]
. In Section 3, we prove Theorem
2
. In Section 4, we prove Theorem
3
. In Section 5, we prove Theorem

1
.

2.
a-priori estimates

In this section, we will derive some a-priori estimates on the strong solution
(Q_{L},v_{L})
of the system (
1.11
)-(
1.13
) in

\mathbb{R}^{3}\times[0,T_{L}]
.

2.1.
Property on the density

In order to obtain a-priori energy estimates, we need to establish some key properties on the density.
Under the condition (
1.7
), one can verify from a result in
[
18
]
that there are two uniform constants
\alpha>0
and
\Lambda>0
such that for any
Q\in\mathbb{M}^{3\times 3}
and
p\in\mathbb{M}^{3\times 3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
f_{E}(Q,p)
also satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha}{2}|p|^{2}\leq f_{E}(Q,p)\leq

\displaystyle\Lambda(1+|Q|^{2})|p|^{2},\quad|\partial_{Q}f_{E}(Q,p)|\leq\Lambda(1+|Q|)|p|^{2},

\displaystyle|\partial^{2}_{Qp}f_{E}(Q,p)|\leq

\displaystyle\Lambda(1+|Q|)|p|,\qquad|\partial^{2}_{pp}f_{E}(Q,p)|\leq\Lambda(1+|Q|^{2}).

(2.1)

Noting that
f_{E}(Q,p)
is
quadratic in
p
and satisfies (
2.1
), one has (c.f.
[
16
]
)

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha}{2}|\xi|^{2}\leq\partial^{2}_{p^{i}_{kl}p^{j}_{mn}}f_{E}(Q,p)\xi^{i}_{kl}\xi^{j}_{mn}\leq\Lambda(1+|Q|^{2})|\xi|^{2},\quad\forall\xi\in\mathbb{M}^{3\times 3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}.

(2.2)

Recall that

\displaystyle S_{\delta}=\left\{Q\in S_{0}:\quad\dist(Q;S_{*})\leq\delta\right\}.

(2.3)

We assume that
\delta>0
is sufficiently small through out this paper. Let
\pi(Q)
be a smooth projection from
S_{\delta}
to
S_{*}
. Then
f_{B}(Q)
satisfies (c.f.
[
25
]
,
[
10
]
)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{2}|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}\leq f_{B}

\displaystyle(Q)\leq C|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}

(2.4)

for some
C>0
.
Since each smooth
\pi(Q)\in S_{*}
has a constant number of distinct eigenvalues, there exists a smooth matrix
R_{Q}:=R(\pi(Q))\in SO(3)
such that
R^{T}_{Q}\pi(Q)R_{Q}
is diagonal (c.f.
[
26
]
). Since
S_{*}
has only three elements of diagonal forms, we can assume without loss of generality that

\displaystyle R^{T}_{Q}\pi(Q)R_{Q}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{-s_{+}}{3}&0&0\\
0&\frac{-s_{+}}{3}&0\\
0&0&\frac{2s_{+}}{3}\end{pmatrix}=:Q^{+}.

(2.5)

Since
\pi(Q)
commutes with
Q
(c.f.
[
25
]
), we have

\displaystyle R^{T}_{Q}QR_{Q}Q^{+}=Q^{+}R^{T}_{Q}QR_{Q}.

(2.6)

Then for any
Q\in S_{\delta}
, it follows from using (
2.5
)-(
2.6
) that

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}=R^{T}_{Q}QR_{Q}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{Q}_{11}&\tilde{Q}_{12}&0\\
\tilde{Q}_{21}&\tilde{Q}_{22}&0\\
0&0&\tilde{Q}_{33}\end{pmatrix}.

(2.7)

Lemma 2.1
.

For any
Q\in S_{\delta}
, let
\tilde{Q}
be defined in (
2.7
). Then the Hessian of the bulk density
f_{B}(Q)
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{2}|\xi|^{2}\leq\partial^{2}_{\tilde{Q}_{ij}\tilde{Q}_{kl}}f_{B}(\tilde{Q})\xi_{ij}\xi_{kl}

(2.8)

for all
\xi\in S_{0}
of the form

\displaystyle\xi=\begin{pmatrix}\xi_{11}&\xi_{12}&0\\
\xi_{21}&\xi_{22}&0\\
0&0&\xi_{33}\end{pmatrix},

(2.9)

where
\lambda=\min\{{s_{+}b},{3a}\}>0
.

Proof.

Calculating second partial derivatives of
f_{B}(\tilde{Q})
with respect to
\tilde{Q}
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{\tilde{i}\tilde{j}}}\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{ij}}f_{B}(\tilde{Q})=

\displaystyle-a\delta_{i\tilde{i}}\delta_{j\tilde{j}}-b(\delta_{\tilde{i}j}\tilde{Q}_{\tilde{j}i}+\delta_{\tilde{j}i}\tilde{Q}_{j\tilde{i}})+c(\delta_{i\tilde{i}}\delta_{j\tilde{j}}|\tilde{Q}|^{2}+2\tilde{Q}_{ij}\tilde{Q}_{\tilde{i}\tilde{j}}).

Note from
[
24
]
that

\displaystyle|Q^{+}|^{2}=\frac{2}{3}s_{+}^{2},\qquad 2cs_{+}^{2}=3a+bs_{+}.

(2.10)

Then we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{\tilde{i}\tilde{j}}}\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{ij}}f_{B}(\tilde{Q})\Big|_{\tilde{Q}=Q^{+}}=

\displaystyle\left(b\Big(\frac{1}{3}s_{+}\delta_{i\tilde{i}}\delta_{j\tilde{j}}-(\delta_{\tilde{i}j}\tilde{Q}_{\tilde{j}i}+\delta_{\tilde{j}i}\tilde{Q}_{j\tilde{i}})\Big)+2c\tilde{Q}_{ij}\tilde{Q}_{\tilde{i}\tilde{j}}\right)\Big|_{\tilde{Q}=Q^{+}}.

(2.11)

In the case of
i=j=\tilde{i}=\tilde{j}
in (
2.11
), we apply the relation (
2.10
) to obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{11}}\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{11}}f_{B}(Q^{+})=

\displaystyle\left(s_{+}b+\frac{2s_{+}^{2}}{9}c\right)=\frac{1}{3}a+\frac{10s_{+}}{9}b=\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{22}}\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{22}}f_{B}(Q^{+}),

(2.12)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{33}}\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{33}}f_{B}(Q^{+})=

\displaystyle-s_{+}b+\frac{8s_{+}^{2}}{9}c=\frac{4}{3}a-\frac{5s_{+}}{9}b.

(2.13)

For the case of
i=j\neq\tilde{i}=\tilde{j}
in (
2.11
), we compute

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{11}}\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{22}}f_{B}(Q^{+})=

\displaystyle 2cQ^{+}_{11}Q^{+}_{22}=\frac{2s_{+}^{2}}{9}c=\frac{1}{3}a+\frac{s_{+}}{9}b,

(2.14)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{11}}\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{33}}f_{B}(Q^{+})=

\displaystyle 2cQ^{+}_{11}Q^{+}_{33}=-\left(\frac{2}{3}a+\frac{2s_{+}}{9}b\right)=\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{22}}\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{33}}f_{B}(Q^{+}).

(2.15)

For the remaining cases of either
i\neq j
or
\tilde{i}\neq\tilde{j}
in (
2.11
), we find

\displaystyle\left.\left(\sum_{i\neq j}\sum_{\tilde{i},\tilde{j}}+\sum_{\tilde{i}\neq\tilde{j}}\sum_{i,j}\right)\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{\tilde{i}\tilde{j}}}\partial_{\tilde{Q}_{ij}}f_{B}(\tilde{Q})\xi_{\tilde{i}\tilde{j}}\xi_{ij}\right|_{\tilde{Q}=Q^{+}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i\neq j}\sum_{\tilde{i},\tilde{j}}+\sum_{\tilde{i}\neq\tilde{j}}\sum_{i,j}\right)\left(2cQ^{+}_{ij}Q^{+}_{\tilde{i}\tilde{j}}+b(\frac{1}{3}s_{+}\delta_{i\tilde{i}}\delta_{j\tilde{j}}-\delta_{\tilde{i}j}Q^{+}_{\tilde{j}i}-\delta_{\tilde{j}i}Q^{+}_{j\tilde{i}})\right)\xi_{\tilde{i}\tilde{j}}\xi_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i\neq j}b\left(\frac{1}{3}s_{+}-Q^{+}_{ii}-Q^{+}_{jj}\right)\xi_{ij}^{2}=s_{+}b(\xi_{12}^{2}+\xi_{21}^{2}),

(2.16)

where we employed (
2.10
) in the last step.
Using the relations (
2.12
)-(
2.16
) with the fact that
\tr(\xi)=0
and (
2.9
), we have

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{\tilde{Q}_{ij}\tilde{Q}_{kl}}f_{B}(Q^{+})\xi_{ij}\xi_{kl}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{3}a+\frac{10s_{+}}{9}b\right)(\xi_{11}^{2}+\xi_{22}^{2})+\left(\frac{2}{3}a+\frac{2s_{+}}{9}b\right)(\xi_{11}\xi_{22})+2s_{+}b\xi_{12}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{4}{3}a-\frac{5s_{+}}{9}b\right)\xi_{33}^{2}-\left(\frac{4}{3}a+\frac{4s_{+}}{9}b\right)\xi_{33}(\xi_{11}+\xi_{22})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle s_{+}b(\xi_{11}^{2}+\xi_{22}^{2})+({\frac{8}{3}}a-\frac{s_{+}}{9}b)\xi_{33}^{2}+2s_{+}b\xi_{12}^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{3}a+\frac{s_{+}}{9}b\right)(\xi_{11}+\xi_{22})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle s_{+}b(\xi_{11}^{2}+\xi_{22}^{2}+\xi_{12}^{2}+\xi_{21}^{2})+3a\xi_{33}^{2}\geq\lambda|\xi|^{2},

(2.17)

where
\lambda=\min\{{s_{+}b},{3a}\}>0
.

Due to the continuity of second derivatives of
f_{B}(\tilde{Q})
and the fact that
|\tilde{Q}-Q^{+}|=\dist(Q;S_{*})\leq\delta
with sufficiently small
\delta
, the claim (
2.8
) follows from using (
2.17
).
∎

Corollary 1
.

For any
Q\in S_{\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{2}|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{\tilde{Q}_{ij}\tilde{Q}_{kl}}f_{B}(\tilde{Q})\nabla\tilde{Q}_{ij}\nabla\tilde{Q}_{kl}+C|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}|\nabla Q|^{2}.

(2.18)

Moreover, for any
k\geq 2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{2}|\nabla^{k}(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\partial^{2}_{\tilde{Q}_{ij}\tilde{Q}_{kl}}f_{B}(\tilde{Q})\nabla^{k}\tilde{Q}_{ij}\nabla^{k}\tilde{Q}_{kl}

\displaystyle+C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu_{1}\leq k-1\\
\mu_{1}+\cdots+\mu_{k+1}=k\end{subarray}}|\nabla^{\mu_{1}}(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}|\nabla^{\mu_{2}}Q|^{2}\cdots|\nabla^{\mu_{k+1}}Q|^{2}.

(2.19)

Proof.

Taking
\xi=\nabla^{k}\tilde{Q}
for
k\geq 1
in Lemma
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{3\lambda}{4}|\nabla^{k}\tilde{Q}|^{2}\leq\partial^{2}_{\tilde{Q}_{ij}\tilde{Q}_{kl}}f_{B}(\tilde{Q})\nabla^{k}\tilde{Q}_{ij}\nabla^{k}\tilde{Q}_{kl}.

(2.20)

We note

\displaystyle|\nabla\tilde{Q}|^{2}=|\nabla(\tilde{Q}-Q^{+})|^{2}=|\nabla(R_{Q}^{T}(Q-\pi(Q))R_{Q})|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2}{3}|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}-C|Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}|\nabla R_{Q}|^{2}.

(2.21)

Since
R_{Q}
is smooth for
Q\in S_{\delta}
, the inequality (
2.18
) follows from (
2.20
)-(
2.21
).

For any
k\geq 2
, we expand the left-hand side of (
2.20
) to show

\displaystyle\frac{3\lambda}{4}|\nabla^{k}\tilde{Q}|^{2}=\frac{3\lambda}{4}|\nabla^{k}(R_{Q}^{T}(Q-\pi(Q))R_{Q})|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{2}|\nabla^{k}(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}-C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+\mu_{3}=k\\
\mu_{2}<k\end{subarray}}|\nabla^{\mu_{1}}R_{Q}^{T}|^{2}|\nabla^{\mu_{2}}(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}|\nabla^{\mu_{3}}R_{Q}|^{2}.

(2.22)

Since
R_{Q}
is smooth for
Q\in S_{\delta}
, we find

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}R_{Q}|\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\sum_{\mu_{1}+\cdots+\mu_{k}=k}|\nabla^{\mu_{1}}Q|\cdots|\nabla^{\mu_{k}}Q|\right).

(2.23)

Then applying (
2.23
) and (
2.22
) to (
2.20
), we prove (
2.19
).
∎

Lemma 2.2
.

For any
Q\in S_{\delta}
, let
\tilde{Q}
be defined in (
2.7
). Then

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla^{k}g_{B}(\tilde{Q}),\nabla R_{Q}^{T}\pi(Q)R_{Q}-R_{Q}^{T}\pi(Q)\nabla R_{Q}\right\rangle=0.

(2.24)

Proof.

For a fixed
Q_{0}\in S_{\delta}
, there exists
R_{0}=R(\pi(Q_{0}))\in SO(3)
such that
R_{0}^{T}\pi(Q_{0})R_{0}=Q^{+}
is a diagonal matrix. Set
\tilde{R}(Q)=R_{0}^{T}R_{Q}
. Since
\tilde{R}(Q_{0})=I
, a direct calculation shows that

\displaystyle\nabla\tilde{R}_{ij}(Q_{0})+\nabla\tilde{R}_{ji}(Q_{0})=0,\quad\forall i,j=1,2,3.

(2.25)

Then we obtain

\displaystyle\left.\left\langle\nabla^{k}g_{B}(\tilde{Q}),\nabla R_{Q}^{T}\pi(Q)R_{Q}-R_{Q}^{T}\pi(Q)\nabla R_{Q}\right\rangle\right|_{Q=Q_{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla^{k}g_{B}(\tilde{Q}_{0}),\nabla\tilde{R}^{T}(Q_{0})(R_{0}^{T}\pi(Q_{0})R_{0})\tilde{R}(Q_{0})-\tilde{R}^{T}(Q)(R_{0}^{T}\pi(Q_{0})R_{0})\nabla\tilde{R}(Q_{0})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla^{k}g_{B}(\tilde{Q}_{0}),\nabla\tilde{R}^{T}(Q_{0})Q^{+}-Q^{+}\nabla\tilde{R}(Q_{0})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j=1}^{3}(\nabla^{k}g_{B}(\tilde{Q}_{0}))_{ij}\left(\nabla\tilde{R}_{ji}(Q_{0})Q^{+}_{jj}+Q^{+}_{ii}\nabla\tilde{R}_{ij}(Q_{0})\right).

Using (
2.25
) and the fact that
Q^{+}_{11}=Q^{+}_{22}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}(\nabla^{k}g_{B}(\tilde{Q}_{0}))_{ij}\left(\nabla\tilde{R}_{ji}(Q_{0})Q^{+}_{jj}+Q^{+}_{ii}\nabla\tilde{R}_{ij}(Q_{0})\right)=0.

(2.26)

It follows from (
1.15
) and (
2.7
) that
\tilde{Q}_{13}=\tilde{Q}_{23}=0
for each
Q\in S_{\delta}
. Thus

\displaystyle(g_{B}(\tilde{Q}))_{13}=

\displaystyle a(\tilde{Q})_{13}+b\sum_{k=1}^{3}\tilde{Q}_{1k}\tilde{Q}_{k3}-c(\tilde{Q})_{13}\tr(\tilde{Q}^{2})=0.

Then
\nabla^{k}(g_{B}(\tilde{Q}))_{13}=0
for any
Q\in S_{\delta}
. Similarly,
\nabla^{k}(g_{B}(\tilde{Q}))_{23}=0
. For the case of
i=3
, we apply (
2.25
) to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}(\nabla^{k}g_{B}(\tilde{Q}_{0}))_{3j}\left(\nabla\tilde{R}_{ji}(Q_{0})Q^{+}_{jj}+Q^{+}_{33}\nabla\tilde{R}_{3j}(Q)\right)\

\displaystyle=2(\nabla^{k}g_{B}(\tilde{Q}_{0}))_{33}\nabla\tilde{R}_{33}(Q_{0})Q^{+}_{33}=0.

(2.27)

Similarly, we can prove it for
j=3
. In view of (
2.26
) and (
2.27
), we prove the claim (
2.24
) for any
Q=Q_{0}\in S_{\delta}
.

∎

2.2.
Some a-priori estimates

For simplicity of notations, we denote
f_{E}(Q,\nabla Q)
by
f_{E}
and only write the subscript
L
in the statement
of each lemma, but omit it in all proofs in this section.

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
F
be a
3\times 3
matrix. For any symmetric
A,B
matrices, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle[A,F],B\right\rangle=\left\langle F,[A,B]\right\rangle=-\left\langle F^{T},[A,B]\right\rangle.

(2.28)

Proof.

Note the following identity

\displaystyle\left\langle[A,F],B\right\rangle=

\displaystyle\left\langle(AF-FA),B\right\rangle=\tr\left((AF)^{T}B-(FA)^{T}B\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tr\left(F^{T}A^{T}B-A^{T}(F^{T}B)\right)=\tr\left(F^{T}A^{T}B-(F^{T}B)A^{T}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle F,[A^{T},B]\right\rangle=\left\langle F,[A,B]\right\rangle.

For the second identity in (
2.28
), we observe that

\left\langle F,[A,B]\right\rangle=\left\langle F^{T},[A,B]^{T}\right\rangle=-\left\langle F^{T},(A^{T}B^{T}-B^{T}A^{T})\right\rangle=-\left\langle F^{T},[A,B]\right\rangle.

Here we used the fact that
A,B
are symmetric in the last step.
∎

Now, we show the following energy identity:

Lemma 2.4
.

Let
(Q_{L},v_{L})
be a strong solution to the system (
1.11
)-(
1.13
) in
\mathbb{R}^{3}\times(0,T_{L})
with the initial condition
(Q_{L,0},v_{L,0})\in H^{2}_{Q_{e}}(\mathbb{R}^{3};S_{*})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3};\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
\div v_{L,0}=0
. Then, for any
s\in(0,T_{L})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(f_{E}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})+\frac{1}{L}f_{B}(Q_{L})+\frac{|v_{L}|^{2}}{2}\right)(x,s)\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left|\mathcal{H}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q_{L})\right|^{2}\,dxdt+\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v_{L}|^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(f_{E}(Q_{L,0},\nabla Q_{L,0})+\frac{1}{L}f_{B}(Q_{L,0})+\frac{|v_{L,0}|^{2}}{2}\Big)\,dx.

(2.29)

Proof.

Taking
L^{2}
inner product of (
1.11
) with
v
and using integration by part yield

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{\,dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v|^{2}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{p_{kl}^{j}}f_{E}\nabla_{i}Q_{kl}\nabla_{j}v_{i}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[Q,\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)]_{ij}\nabla_{j}v_{i}\,dx.

(2.30)

Next, multiplying (
1.13
) with
(\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q))
gives

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\partial_{t}Q,\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q)\right\rangle\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q)|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle(v\cdot\nabla)Q+[Q,\Omega],\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q)\right\rangle\,dx.

(2.31)

In view of (
1.14
) and the relation that
g_{B}(Q)=-\nabla_{Q}f_{B}(Q)-\frac{b}{3}\tr(Q^{2})I
, we have

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\partial_{t}Q,\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q)\right\rangle\,dx=\frac{d}{\,dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(f_{E}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}f_{B}(Q))\,dx.

(2.32)

Utilizing (
1.14
), (
1.15
) and integrating by parts, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle(v\cdot\nabla)Q,\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q)\right\rangle\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle(v\cdot\nabla)Q,\nabla_{j}\big(\partial_{p^{j}}f_{E}\big)-\partial_{Q}f_{E}\right\rangle\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle(v\cdot\nabla)Q,\frac{1}{L}\partial_{Q}f_{B}(Q)\right\rangle\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla_{j}v_{i}\nabla_{i}Q_{kl}\partial_{p_{kl}^{j}}f_{E}+v_{i}\left(\nabla^{2}_{ij}Q_{kl}\partial_{p_{kl}^{j}}f_{E}-\nabla_{i}Q_{kl}\partial_{Q_{kl}}f_{E}-\frac{1}{L}\nabla_{i}f_{B}(Q)\right)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{p_{kl}^{j}}f_{E}\nabla_{i}Q_{kl}\nabla_{j}v_{i}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{i}\nabla_{i}f\,dx=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{p^{j}_{kl}}f_{E}\nabla_{i}Q_{kl}\nabla_{j}v_{i}\,dx,

(2.33)

where we have used
\tr Q=0
in the second equality.

Choosing
A=Q,B=\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q),F=\nabla v
in Lemma
2.3
and using the fact that
[Q,g_{B}]=0
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle[Q,\Omega],\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q)\right\rangle=\nabla_{j}v_{i}[Q,\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)]_{ij}.

(2.34)

Integrating (
2.34
) in
x
and
substituting (
2.32
)-(
2.33
) into (
2.31
) give

\displaystyle\frac{d}{\,dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f(Q,\nabla Q)\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q)|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla_{l}v_{k}[Q,\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)]_{kl}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{p_{kl}^{j}}f_{E}\nabla_{i}Q_{kl}\nabla_{j}v_{i}\,dx.

(2.35)

Therefore, the energy identity (
2.29
) follows from taking the sum of (
2.30
) and (
2.35
) and integrating over the
time interval
[0,s]
.
∎

We rotate the equation (
1.13
) by
R_{Q_{L}}=R(\pi(Q_{L}))
; i.e.

\displaystyle R^{T}_{Q_{L}}\big(\partial_{t}Q_{L}+(v_{L}\cdot\nabla Q_{L})+[Q_{L},\Omega_{L}]\big)R_{Q_{L}}=R^{T}_{Q_{L}}\mathcal{H}(Q_{L},\nabla Q_{L})R_{Q_{L}}+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(\tilde{Q}_{L}),

(2.36)

where we use the fact that
R_{Q_{L}}^{T}g_{B}(Q_{L})R_{Q_{L}}=g_{B}(\tilde{Q}_{L})
.

The strong solutions also admit the following local energy inequality:

Lemma 2.5
.

Let
(Q_{L},v_{L})
be a strong solution to the system (
1.11
)-(
1.13
) in
\mathbb{R}^{3}\times(0,T_{L})
. Assume that
Q\in S_{\delta}
for sufficiently small
\delta
on
\mathbb{R}^{3}\times(0,T_{L})
. Then, for any
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
s\in(0,T_{L})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla Q_{L}|^{2}+|v_{L}|^{2}+\frac{|Q_{L}-\pi(Q_{L})|^{2}}{L}\right)(x,s)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla^{2}Q_{L}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{L}|^{2}+|\partial_{t}Q_{L}|^{2}+\frac{|\nabla(Q_{L}-\pi(Q_{L}))|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla Q_{L,0}|^{2}+|v_{L,0}|^{2}+\frac{|Q_{L,0}-\pi(Q_{L,0})|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla Q_{L}|^{2}\left(|\nabla Q_{L}|^{2}+|v_{L}|^{2}+\frac{|Q_{L}-\pi(Q_{L})|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|P_{L}-c_{L}^{*}(t)||v_{L}||\nabla\phi||\phi|+(|\nabla Q_{L}|^{2}+|v_{L}|^{2})|\nabla\phi|^{2}\,dxdt.

(2.37)

Proof.

Differentiating (
2.36
) and multiplying by
R_{Q}^{T}\nabla QR_{Q}\phi^{2}
yield

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla\left(R^{T}_{Q}(\partial_{t}Q+v\cdot\nabla Q+[Q,\Omega])R_{Q}\right),R_{Q}^{T}\nabla_{\beta}QR_{Q}\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla(R^{T}_{Q}\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)R_{Q})+\frac{1}{L}\nabla g_{B}(\tilde{Q}),R_{Q}^{T}\nabla_{\beta}QR_{Q}\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx.

(2.38)

We observe that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla(R^{T}_{Q}\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)R_{Q}),R_{Q}^{T}\nabla_{\beta}QR_{Q}\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q),\nabla_{\beta}Q\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla R_{Q}||\nabla Q|||\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)|\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla_{\beta}\nabla_{k}(\partial_{p_{ij}^{k}}f_{E})\nabla_{\beta}Q_{ij}\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)||\nabla Q|^{2}+|\partial_{Q}f_{E}(Q,\nabla Q)||\nabla^{2}Q|)\phi^{2}\,dx.

(2.39)

Noting the condition (
2.2
) on
f_{E}
and integrating by parts, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla_{\beta}\nabla_{k}(\partial_{p_{ij}^{k}}f_{E})\nabla_{\beta}Q_{ij}\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla_{k}\left(\partial^{2}_{p_{ij}^{k}p_{mn}^{l}}f_{E}\nabla^{2}_{\beta l}Q_{mn}+\partial^{2}_{p_{ij}^{k}Q_{mn}}f_{E}\nabla_{\beta}Q_{mn}\right)\nabla_{\beta}Q_{ij}\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla_{k}(\partial_{p_{ij}^{k}}f_{E})\nabla_{\beta}Q_{ij}\nabla_{\beta}\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{2}_{p_{ij}^{k}p_{mn}^{l}}f_{E}\nabla^{2}_{\beta l}Q_{mn}\nabla^{2}_{k\beta}Q_{ij}\phi^{2}\,dx+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\partial^{2}_{pp}f_{E}||\nabla^{2}Q||\nabla Q||\nabla\phi||\phi|\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(|\nabla(\partial^{2}_{pQ}f_{E})||\nabla Q|+|\partial^{2}_{pQ}f_{E}||\nabla^{2}Q|\Big)|\nabla Q|\phi^{2}+|\nabla(\partial_{p}f_{E})||\nabla Q||\nabla\phi||\phi|\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{3\alpha}{8}|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}\phi^{2}+C\left(|\nabla Q|^{4}\phi^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{2}|\nabla\phi|^{2}\right)\,dx.

(2.40)

Here we used that
|\nabla(\partial^{2}_{pQ}f_{E})|\leq C(|\nabla^{2}Q|+|\nabla Q|^{2})
due to
Q\in S_{\delta}
.

In view of Corollary
1
, Lemma
2.2
with
k=1
and
g_{B}(\tilde{Q})=-\nabla_{\tilde{Q}}f_{B}(\tilde{Q})-\frac{b}{3}\tr(\tilde{Q}^{2})I
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\frac{1}{L}\nabla g_{B}(\tilde{Q})),R_{Q}^{T}\nabla_{\beta}QR_{Q}\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla g_{B}(\tilde{Q}),\nabla\tilde{Q}-\nabla R_{Q}^{T}QR_{Q}-R_{Q}^{T}Q\nabla R_{Q}\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle-\nabla(\partial_{\tilde{Q}}f_{B}(\tilde{Q})),\nabla\tilde{Q}\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla g_{B}(\tilde{Q}),-\nabla R_{Q}^{T}QR_{Q}-R_{Q}^{T}Q\nabla R_{Q}\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{2}_{\tilde{Q}_{ij}\tilde{Q}_{kl}}f_{B}(\tilde{Q})\nabla\tilde{Q}_{ij}\nabla\tilde{Q}_{kl}\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla g_{B}(\tilde{Q}),\nabla R_{Q}^{T}(\pi(Q)-Q)R_{Q}+R_{Q}^{T}(\pi(Q)-Q)\nabla R_{Q}\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}\phi^{2}\,dx+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla Q|^{2}\frac{|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}}{L}\phi^{2}\,dx,

(2.41)

where we also used that

|\nabla g_{B}(\tilde{Q})|\leq C|\nabla(\tilde{Q}-Q^{+})|\leq C|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|+C|Q-\pi(Q)||\nabla Q|.

Combining (
2.40
), (
2.39
) with (
2.41
) yields

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla(R^{T}_{Q}(\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)R_{Q})+\frac{1}{L}\nabla g_{B}(\tilde{Q}),R_{Q}^{T}\nabla_{\beta}QR_{Q}\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{\alpha}{4}|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{4}\frac{|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla Q|^{2}|\nabla\phi|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{2}\left(|\nabla Q|^{2}+|v|^{2}+\frac{|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx.

(2.42)

Integrating by parts, we estimate the left-hand side of (
2.38
) to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla_{\beta}\left(R^{T}_{Q}(\partial_{t}Q+v\cdot\nabla Q+[Q,\Omega])R_{Q}\right),R_{Q}^{T}\nabla_{\beta}QR_{Q}\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla_{\beta}\partial_{t}Q+(v\cdot\nabla)Q,\nabla_{\beta}Q\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle[Q,\Omega],\nabla_{\beta}(\nabla_{\beta}Q\phi^{2})\right\rangle\,dx

\displaystyle-C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla Q|^{2}\left(|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+|v|^{2}|\nabla Q|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{\,dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla Q|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\frac{\alpha}{8}|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+C|\nabla v|^{2}\Big)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle-C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla Q|^{2}|v|^{2}\phi^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{2}|\nabla\phi|^{2}\,dx.

(2.43)

Adding (
2.42
) to (
2.43
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{\,dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla Q|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{\alpha}{4}|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}\frac{|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+C|\nabla v|^{2}\Big)\phi^{2}\,dx+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla Q|^{2}|\nabla\phi|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla Q|^{2}\left(|\nabla Q|^{2}+|v|^{2}+\frac{|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx.

(2.44)

Multiplying (
1.13
) by
\partial_{t}Q\phi^{2}
and using (
2.32
) in Lemma
2.4
yield

\displaystyle\frac{d}{\,dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(f_{E}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}f_{B}(Q))\phi^{2}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}Q_{ij}\partial_{p^{k}_{ij}}f_{E}\nabla_{k}\phi\phi\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle(v\cdot\nabla)Q+[Q,\Omega],\partial_{t}Q\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{4}|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+C|\nabla v|^{2}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla Q|^{2}|v|^{2}\phi^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{2}|\nabla\phi|^{2}\,dx.

(2.45)

Adding (
2.44
) to (
2.45
), integrating in
t
and using (
2.4
), we see

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla Q|^{2}+\frac{|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}}{L}\right)(x,s)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+\int^{s}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+\frac{|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla Q_{0}|^{2}+\frac{|Q_{0}-\pi(Q_{0})|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int^{s}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dxdt+C\int^{s}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla Q|^{2}|\nabla\phi|^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle+\int^{s}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla Q|^{2}\left(|\nabla Q|^{2}+|v|^{2}+\frac{|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dxdt.

(2.46)

Estimating the term
\nabla v
on the right-hand side of (
2.46
),
we multiply (
1.11
) by
v\phi^{2}
and (
1.13
) by
\Big(\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q)\Big)\phi^{2}
. Then it follows from using the same argument in (
2.30
)-(
2.35
) that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{\,dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{2}|v|^{2}+f_{E}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}f_{B}(Q)\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q)|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|v|^{2}+2\left(P-c^{\ast}(t)\right)\right)v\cdot\nabla\phi\phi-2\nabla_{k}v_{i}v_{i}\nabla_{k}\phi\phi\,dx

\displaystyle-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[Q,\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)]_{ij}v_{i}\nabla_{j}\phi\phi\,dx+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{p_{kl}^{j}}f_{E}\nabla_{i}Q_{kl}v_{i}\nabla_{j}\phi\phi\,dx

\displaystyle-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}Q_{kl}\partial_{p^{j}_{kl}}f_{E}\nabla_{j}\phi\phi\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v\cdot\nabla f\phi^{2}\,dx-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{i}\nabla_{i}Q_{kl}\partial_{p_{kl}^{j}}f_{E}\nabla_{j}\phi\phi\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2})\phi^{2}\,dx+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla Q|^{4}+|v|^{4})\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla Q|^{2}+|v|^{2})|\nabla\phi|^{2}\,dx+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|P_{L}-c_{L}^{*}(t)||v_{L}||\nabla\phi||\phi|\,dx.

(2.47)

Integrating (
2.47
) in
t
, employing (
2.46
) and choosing sufficiently small
\eta
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v(x,s)|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx+\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla Q_{0}|^{2}+|v_{0}|^{2}+\frac{|Q_{0}-\pi(Q_{0})|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla Q|^{4}+|v|^{4})\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla Q|^{2}+|v|^{2})|\nabla\phi|^{2}\,dx+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|P_{L}-c_{L}^{*}(t)||v_{L}||\nabla\phi||\phi|\,dx.

(2.48)

Applying (
2.48
) to (
2.46
), we prove (
2.37
).
∎

Through Corollary
1
and the equation (
2.36
), we obtain second order estimates of
(\nabla Q_{L},v_{L})
in the following:

Lemma 2.6
.

Let
(Q_{L},v_{L})
be a strong solution to the system (
1.11
)-(
1.13
) in
\mathbb{R}^{3}\times(0,T_{L})
. Assume that
Q\in S_{\delta}
for sufficiently small
\delta
on
\mathbb{R}^{3}\times(0,T_{L})
. Then for any
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
s\in(0,T_{L})
, we have the following local estimate

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla^{2}Q_{L}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{L}|^{2}+\frac{|\nabla(Q_{L}-\pi(Q_{L}))|^{2}}{L}\right)(x,s)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla^{3}Q_{L}|^{2}+|\nabla^{2}v_{L}|^{2}+|\nabla\partial_{t}Q_{L}|^{2}+\frac{|\nabla^{2}(Q_{L}-\pi(Q_{L}))|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla^{2}Q_{L,0}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{L,0}|^{2}+\frac{|\nabla(Q_{L,0}-\pi(Q_{L,0}))|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|Q_{L,0}-\pi(Q_{L,0})|^{2}}{L}|\nabla Q_{L,0}|^{2}\phi^{2}+\left(\frac{|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}}{L}|\nabla Q|^{2}\right)(x,s)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e(Q_{L},v_{L})\left(|\nabla^{2}Q_{L}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{L}|^{2}+|\partial_{t}Q_{L}|^{2}\right)\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e(Q_{L},v_{L})\left(\frac{|\nabla(Q_{L}-\pi(Q_{L}))|^{2}}{L}+e^{2}(Q_{L},v_{L})\right)\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(e^{2}(Q_{L},v_{L})+|P_{L}-c_{L}^{*}(t)|^{2}\right)(|\nabla\phi|^{2}+|\nabla^{2}\phi||\phi|)\,dxdt

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla^{2}Q_{L}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{L}|^{2}+|\partial_{t}Q_{L}|^{2}\right)(|\nabla\phi|^{2}+|\nabla^{2}\phi||\phi|)\,dxdt.

(2.49)

Here we denote

e(Q_{L},v_{L}):=|\nabla Q_{L}|^{2}+|v_{L}|^{2}+\frac{|Q_{L}-\pi(Q_{L})|^{2}}{L}.

Proof.

Differentiating (
2.36
) with respect to
x_{\beta}
and
x_{\gamma}
, we multiply by
\nabla_{\beta}(R^{T}_{Q}\nabla_{\gamma}QR_{Q})\phi^{2}
to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla^{2}_{\beta_{\gamma}}\Big(R^{T}_{Q}\big(\partial_{t}Q+v\cdot\nabla Q+[Q,\Omega]\big)R_{Q}\Big),\nabla_{\beta}(R^{T}_{Q}\nabla_{\gamma}QR_{Q})\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla^{2}_{\beta_{\gamma}}\left(R^{T}_{Q}\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)R_{Q}+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(\tilde{Q})\right),\nabla_{\beta}(R^{T}_{Q}\nabla_{\gamma}QR_{Q})\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx.

(2.50)

Integrating by parts twice and using (
2.2
), we estimate

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla^{2}_{\beta\gamma}\nabla_{k}\left(\partial^{2}_{p^{k}}f_{E}\right),\nabla^{2}_{\beta\gamma}Q\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla_{\gamma}\left(\partial^{2}_{p_{ij}^{k}p_{mn}^{l}}f_{E}\nabla^{2}_{\beta l}Q_{mn}+\partial^{2}_{p_{ij}^{k}Q_{mn}}f_{E}\nabla_{\beta}Q_{mn}\right)\nabla_{k}(\nabla^{2}_{\beta\gamma}Q_{ij}\phi^{2})\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla_{\gamma}\left(\partial^{2}_{p_{ij}^{k}p_{mn}^{l}}f_{E}\nabla^{2}_{\beta l}Q_{mn}\right)\nabla^{3}_{\beta\gamma k}Q_{ij}\phi^{2}\,dx-C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\partial^{2}_{pp}f_{E}||\nabla^{2}Q||\nabla^{3}Q||\nabla\phi||\phi|\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla\partial^{2}_{pQ}f_{E}||\nabla Q|+|\partial^{2}_{pQ}f_{E}|\nabla^{2}Q|)|\nabla^{3}Q|\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{2}_{p_{ij}^{k}p_{mn}^{l}}f_{E}\nabla^{3}_{\beta l\gamma}Q_{mn}\nabla^{3}_{\beta\gamma k}Q_{ij}\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla^{2}Q||\nabla Q|+|\nabla Q|^{3})|\nabla^{3}Q|\phi^{2}+|\nabla^{2}Q||\nabla^{3}Q||\nabla\phi||\phi|\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{3\alpha}{8}|\nabla^{3}Q|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla Q|^{2}(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4})\phi^{2}+|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}|\nabla\phi|^{2}\,dx.

(2.51)

Then using (
2.51
) and integrating by parts, we find

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla^{2}_{\beta\gamma}\left(R^{T}_{Q}\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)R_{Q}\right),\nabla_{\beta}(R^{T}_{Q}\nabla_{\gamma}QR_{Q})\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla^{2}_{\beta\gamma}\left(\nabla_{k}\partial_{p^{k}}f_{E}-\partial_{Q}f_{E}\right),\nabla^{2}_{\beta\gamma}Q\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)|(|\nabla R_{Q}||\nabla^{2}Q|+|\nabla^{2}R_{Q}||\nabla Q|+|\nabla R_{Q}|^{2}|\nabla Q|)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla R_{Q}||\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)||\nabla^{2}(R^{T}_{Q}\nabla QR_{Q})|\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\alpha}{4}|\nabla^{3}Q|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4})(|\nabla Q|^{2}\phi^{2}+|\nabla\phi|^{2})\,dx.

(2.52)

Here we used that
|\nabla^{2}(R^{T}_{Q}\nabla QR_{Q})|+|\nabla\mathcal{H}|\leq C(|\nabla^{3}Q|+|\nabla^{2}Q||\nabla Q|+|\nabla Q|^{3})
.

Applying Corollary
1
, Lemma
2.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla^{2}_{\beta\gamma}\frac{g_{B}(\tilde{Q})}{L},\nabla_{\beta}(R^{T}_{Q}\nabla_{\gamma}QR_{Q})\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla_{\beta}\left(\phi^{2}\nabla^{2}_{\beta\gamma}\frac{(g_{B}(\tilde{Q}))_{ij}}{L}\right)\nabla_{\gamma}\tilde{Q}_{ij}\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla_{\beta}\left(\phi^{2}\nabla^{2}_{\beta\gamma}\frac{(g_{B}(\tilde{Q}))_{ij}}{L}\right)\left(\nabla_{\gamma}R_{Q}^{T}(Q-\pi(Q))R_{Q}+R_{Q}^{T}(Q-\pi(Q))\nabla_{\gamma}R_{Q}\right)_{ij}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{2}_{\tilde{Q}_{ij}\tilde{Q}_{kl}}f_{B}(\tilde{Q})\nabla^{2}_{\beta\gamma}\tilde{Q}_{kl}\nabla^{2}_{\beta\gamma}\tilde{Q}_{ij}\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\partial^{3}_{\tilde{Q}}f_{B}(\tilde{Q})||\nabla Q|\frac{|\nabla\tilde{Q}|}{L^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{|\nabla^{2}\tilde{Q}|}{L^{\frac{1}{2}}}\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|\nabla^{2}(g_{B}(\tilde{Q}))|}{L}\left((|\nabla^{2}Q|+|\nabla Q|^{2})|Q-\pi(Q)|+|\nabla Q||\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|\nabla^{2}(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}\phi^{2}\,dx+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}|\nabla Q|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}}{L}(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4})\phi^{2}\,dx,

(2.53)

where we used that

|\nabla^{2}(g_{B}(\tilde{Q}))|\leq C(|\nabla^{2}(Q-\pi(Q))|+|\nabla Q||\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|+(|\nabla^{2}Q|+|\nabla Q|^{2})|Q-\pi(Q)|.

We compute the left-hand side of (
2.50
) to get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla^{2}_{\beta\gamma}\Big(R^{T}_{Q}\big(\partial_{t}Q+v\cdot\nabla Q+[Q,\Omega]\big)R_{Q}\Big),\nabla_{\beta}(R^{T}_{Q}\nabla_{\gamma}QR_{Q})\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla^{2}_{\beta\gamma}\partial_{t}Q,\nabla^{2}_{\beta\gamma}Q\right\rangle\phi^{2}+\left\langle\nabla_{\beta}\partial_{t}Q,\nabla^{2}_{\beta\gamma}Q\right\rangle\nabla_{\gamma}(\phi^{2})\,dx

\displaystyle-C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla\partial_{t}Q|(|\nabla^{2}Q||\nabla R_{Q}|+|\nabla Q||\nabla^{2}R_{Q}|+|\nabla Q||\nabla R_{Q}|^{2})\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle-C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(|\partial_{t}Q||\nabla Q|+|v|(|\nabla^{2}Q|+|\nabla Q|^{2})\Big)|\nabla^{2}(R^{T}_{Q}\nabla QR_{Q})|\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle-C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(|\nabla v||\nabla Q|+|\nabla^{2}v|\Big)|\nabla^{2}(R^{T}_{Q}\nabla QR_{Q})|\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{4}|\nabla\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+\frac{\alpha}{8}|\nabla^{3}Q|^{2}+C|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle-C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2}+|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4})(|\nabla Q|^{2}+|v|^{2})\phi^{2}+|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}|\nabla\phi|^{2}\,dx.

(2.54)

In view of (
2.50
)-(
2.54
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{\alpha}{8}|\nabla^{3}Q|^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{4}\frac{|\nabla^{2}(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{4}|\nabla\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+C|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e(Q,v)\left(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2}+|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4}+\frac{|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(e^{2}(Q,v)+|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}\right)|\nabla\phi|^{2}\,dx.

(2.55)

Estimating the term on
\nabla\partial_{t}Q
in (
2.55
), we differentiate (
2.36
) in
x_{\beta}
and multiply it by
\nabla_{\beta}(R^{T}_{Q}\partial_{t}QR_{Q})\phi^{2}
to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla_{\beta}\left(R^{T}_{Q}(\partial_{t}Q+v\cdot\nabla Q+[Q,\Omega])R_{Q}\right),\nabla_{\beta}(R^{T}_{Q}\partial_{t}QR_{Q})\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla_{\beta}\left((R^{T}_{Q}\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)R_{Q})+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(\tilde{Q})\right),\nabla_{\beta}(R^{T}_{Q}\partial_{t}QR_{Q})\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dxdt.

(2.56)

Using (
2.2
), we derive

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla_{\beta}(R^{T}_{Q}\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)R_{Q}),\nabla_{\beta}(R^{T}_{Q}\partial_{t}QR_{Q})\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla_{\beta}(\nabla_{k}\partial_{p^{k}}f_{E}-\partial_{Q}f_{E}),\nabla_{\beta}\partial_{t}Q\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla\mathcal{H}||\nabla R_{Q}||\partial_{t}Q|+|\nabla R_{Q}||\mathcal{H}||\nabla(R^{T}_{Q}\partial_{t}QR_{Q})|)\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\Big(\partial^{2}_{p_{ij}^{k}p_{mn}^{l}}f_{E}\nabla^{2}_{\beta l}Q_{mn}\nabla^{2}_{\beta k}Q_{ij}\Big)\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\partial^{2}_{p_{ij}^{k}p_{mn}^{l}}f_{E}\nabla^{2}_{\beta l}Q_{mn}\nabla^{2}_{\beta k}Q_{ij}\phi^{2}+C|\partial^{2}_{pp}f_{E}||\nabla^{2}Q||\nabla\partial_{t}Q||\nabla\phi||\phi|\,dxdt

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\partial^{2}_{pQ}f_{E}||\nabla Q|+|\nabla\partial_{Q}f_{E}|)|\nabla\partial_{t}Q|\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(|\nabla\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)||\nabla Q||\partial_{t}Q|\Big)\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)||\nabla Q|(|\nabla\partial_{t}Q|+|\nabla Q||\partial_{t}Q|)\ \phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(C|\nabla^{2}Q_{0}|^{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}|\nabla^{2}Q(x,s)|^{2})\phi^{2}\,dx+\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\frac{\alpha}{16}|\nabla^{3}Q|^{2}+\frac{1}{8}|\nabla\partial_{t}Q|^{2})\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla Q|^{2}(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4})\phi^{2}+(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4})|\nabla\phi|^{2}\,dxdt.

(2.57)

By integrating by parts and using the fact that

|\nabla(R^{T}_{Q}\partial_{t}QR_{Q})|\leq C(|\nabla\partial_{t}Q|+|\partial_{t}Q||\nabla Q|),

we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{2L}\partial_{t}\partial^{2}_{\tilde{Q}_{ij}\tilde{Q}_{kl}}f_{B}(\tilde{Q})\nabla_{\beta}\tilde{Q}_{kl}\nabla_{\beta}\tilde{Q}_{ij}\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|Q-\pi(Q)|\left(|\nabla\partial_{t}Q||\nabla\tilde{Q}|+|\partial_{t}Q||\nabla Q||\nabla\tilde{Q}|+|\partial_{t}Q||\nabla^{2}\tilde{Q}|\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|Q-\pi(Q)|\left(|\partial_{t}Q||\nabla\tilde{Q}|\right)|\nabla\phi||\phi|\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{4}|\nabla\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{16}\frac{|\nabla^{2}(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}+C|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}|\nabla Q|^{2}+C|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}|\nabla\phi|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}\left(|\nabla Q|^{2}+\frac{|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}}{L}\left(\frac{|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}}{L}|\nabla Q|^{2}+|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4}+|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx.

(2.58)

Here in the last inequality, we have used

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}\tilde{Q}|^{2}\leq

\displaystyle C(|\nabla^{2}(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}+|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}|\nabla Q|^{2}+|Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4})).

Utilizing a similar argument to one in Corollary
1
and (
2.58
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\frac{\nabla_{\beta}g_{B}(\tilde{Q})}{L},\nabla_{\beta}(R^{T}_{Q}\partial_{t}QR_{Q})\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\nabla_{\beta}(g_{B}(\tilde{Q}))_{ij}}{L}\nabla_{\beta}\partial_{t}\tilde{Q}_{ij}\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|\nabla^{2}g_{B}(\tilde{Q})|}{L^{\frac{1}{2}}}|\partial_{t}R_{Q}|\frac{|Q-\pi(Q)|}{L^{\frac{1}{2}}}\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{2L}\partial_{t}\left(\partial^{2}_{\tilde{Q}_{ij}\tilde{Q}_{kl}}f_{B}(\tilde{Q})\nabla_{\beta}\tilde{Q}_{ij}\nabla_{\beta}\tilde{Q}_{kl}\right)\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{2L}\partial_{t}\partial^{2}_{\tilde{Q}_{ij}\tilde{Q}_{kl}}f_{B}(\tilde{Q})\nabla_{\beta}\tilde{Q}_{kl}\nabla_{\beta}\tilde{Q}_{ij}\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|\nabla^{2}g_{B}(\tilde{Q})|}{L^{\frac{1}{2}}}|\partial_{t}Q|\frac{|Q-\pi(Q)|}{L^{\frac{1}{2}}}\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(-\frac{\lambda}{4}\frac{|\nabla(Q(x,s)-\pi(Q(x,s)))|^{2}}{L}+C\frac{|\nabla(Q_{0}-\pi(Q_{0}))|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{|Q_{L,0}-\pi(Q_{L,0})|^{2}}{L}|\nabla Q_{L,0}|^{2}+\frac{|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}}{L}|\nabla Q|^{2}\right)(x,s)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{4}|\nabla\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{8}\frac{|\nabla^{2}(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e(Q,v)\left(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+\frac{|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}+e^{2}(Q,v)\right)\phi^{2}\,dxdt.

(2.59)

Applying Young’s inequality to the left-hand side of (
2.56
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla_{\beta}\left(R^{T}_{Q}(\partial_{t}Q+v\cdot\nabla Q+[Q,\Omega])R_{Q}\right),\nabla_{\beta}(R^{T}_{Q}\partial_{t}QR_{Q})\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla_{\beta}\partial_{t}Q,\nabla_{\beta}\partial_{t}Q\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla\partial_{t}Q||\nabla R_{Q}||\partial_{t}Q|\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle-C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(|\partial_{t}Q||\nabla Q|+|v|(|\nabla^{2}Q|+|\nabla Q|^{2})+|\nabla v||\nabla Q|+|\nabla^{2}v|\Big)|\nabla(R^{T}_{Q}\partial_{t}QR_{Q})|\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{3}{4}|\nabla\partial_{t}Q|^{2}\phi^{2}-C|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\phi^{2}-C\left(|\nabla Q|^{2}(|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})+|v|^{2}|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx.

(2.60)

Substituting (
2.57
), (
2.59
) and (
2.60
) into (

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{\alpha}{4}|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{8}\frac{|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}\right)(x,s)\phi^{2}\,dx+\frac{3}{8}\int^{s}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla\partial_{t}Q|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla^{2}Q_{0}|^{2}+\frac{|\nabla(Q_{0}-\pi(Q_{0}))|^{2}}{L}+\frac{|Q_{L,0}-\pi(Q_{L,0})|^{2}}{L}|\nabla Q_{L,0}|^{2}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}}{L}|\nabla Q|^{2}\right)(x,s)\phi^{2}\,dx+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{\alpha}{16}|\nabla^{3}Q|^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{8}\frac{|\nabla^{2}(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2}+|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}\right)(e(Q,v)\phi^{2}+|\nabla\phi|^{2})\,dxdt

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}+e^{2}(Q,v)\right)(e(Q,v)\phi^{2}+|\nabla\phi|^{2})\,dxdt.

(2.61)

Integrating (
2.55
) in
t
then adding it to (
2.61
), we derive

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+\frac{|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}\right)(x,s)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+\int^{s}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla^{3}Q|^{2}+|\nabla\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+\frac{|\nabla^{2}(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}\right)\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla^{2}Q_{0}|^{2}+\frac{|\nabla(Q_{0}-\pi(Q_{0}))|^{2}}{L}+\frac{|Q_{L,0}-\pi(Q_{L,0})|^{2}}{L}|\nabla Q_{L,0}|^{2}\right)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{|Q-\pi(Q)|^{2}}{L}|\nabla Q|^{2}\right)(x,s)\phi^{2}\,dx+\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}C|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dxdt

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2}+|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}\right)(e(Q,v)\phi^{2}+|\nabla\phi|^{2})\,dxdt

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{|\nabla(Q-\pi(Q))|^{2}}{L}+e^{2}(Q,v)\right)(e(Q,v)\phi^{2}+|\nabla\phi|^{2})\,dxdt.

(2.62)

To estimate the term
\nabla^{2}v
in (
2.62
), we take
L^{2}
inner product of (
1.11
) with
-\Delta v\phi^{2}
and calculate

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{\,dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}2\partial_{t}v_{i}\nabla_{j}v_{i}\nabla_{j}\phi\phi\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}2(\nabla_{j}v_{i}\Delta v_{i}-\nabla_{k}v_{i}\nabla_{kj}^{2}v_{i})\nabla_{j}\phi\phi\,dx

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(P-c^{\ast})\Delta v_{i}\nabla_{i}\phi\phi\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla_{j}\sigma_{ij}\Delta v_{i}\phi^{2}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla_{j}[Q,\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)]_{ij}\Delta v_{i}\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla_{j}[Q,\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)]_{ij}\Delta v_{i}\phi^{2}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}2\partial_{t}v_{i}\nabla_{j}v_{i}\nabla_{j}\phi\phi\,dx+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla v|^{2}+|P-c^{\ast}|^{2})|\nabla\phi|^{2}\,dx+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4})|\nabla Q|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx,

where we have used the fact that
|\nabla\sigma(Q,\nabla Q)|\leq C(|\nabla^{2}Q|+|\nabla Q|^{2})|\nabla Q|
.

By using (
1.11
) and integrating by parts, we have

\displaystyle-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}v_{i}\nabla_{j}v_{i}\nabla_{j}\phi\phi\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(v_{k}\nabla_{k}v_{i}-\Delta v_{i}+\nabla_{k}\sigma_{ik})\nabla_{j}v_{i}\nabla_{j}\phi\phi\,dx+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(P-c^{\ast})\nabla_{j}v_{i}\nabla_{i}(\nabla_{j}\phi\phi)\,dx

\displaystyle+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[Q,\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)]_{ik}\Big(\nabla_{kj}^{2}v_{i}\nabla_{j}\phi\phi+\nabla_{j}v_{i}\nabla_{k}(\nabla_{j}\phi\phi)\Big)\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|v|^{2}|\nabla v|^{2}+(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4})|\nabla Q|^{2})\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|P-c^{\ast}|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2}+|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4})(|\nabla^{2}\phi||\phi|+|\nabla\phi|^{2})\,dx,

which, plugging into the previous inequality, yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{\,dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v|^{2}\,dx+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla_{j}[Q,\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)]_{ij}\Delta v_{i}\phi^{2}\,dx+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|P-c^{\ast}|^{2}(|\nabla^{2}\phi||\phi|+|\nabla\phi|^{2})\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4})(|\nabla^{2}\phi||\phi|+|\nabla\phi|^{2})\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla Q|^{2}+|v|^{2})(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4})\phi^{2}\,dx.

(2.63)

Choosing
A=Q,B=\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q),F=\Delta\nabla v
in Lemma
2.3
, we observe

\displaystyle\left\langle[Q,\Delta\Omega],\mathcal{H}+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}\right\rangle=\Delta\nabla_{j}v_{i}[Q,\mathcal{H}]_{ij}.

(2.64)

Then, integrating by parts and using (
2.64
) and (
1.13
) on the term
(\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q))
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla_{\beta}\left(R^{T}[Q,\Omega]R\right),\nabla_{\beta}\left(R^{T}_{Q}(\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q))R_{Q}\right)\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle[\Delta Q,\Omega]+2[\nabla_{\beta}Q,\nabla_{\beta}\Omega]+[Q,\Delta\Omega],\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q)\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla[Q,\Omega]|\Big|\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q)\Big|(|\nabla Q|\phi^{2}+|\nabla\phi||\phi|)\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla Q||\nabla v|\Big|\nabla\left(R^{T}_{Q}\big(\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q)\big)R_{Q}\right)\Big|\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\Delta v_{i}\nabla_{j}[Q,\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)]_{ij}+\eta|\nabla\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\Big)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2}+|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4}+|v|^{4})((|\nabla Q|^{2}+|v|^{2})\phi^{2}+|\nabla\phi|^{2})\,dx

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big|\nabla\left(R^{T}_{Q}\big(\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q)\big)R_{Q}\right)\Big|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx.

(2.65)

Here we used that
|[Q,\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)]|^{2}|\nabla\phi|^{2}\leq C(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4})|\nabla\phi|^{2}
.

We differentiate (
2.36
) with respect to
x_{\beta}
, multiply by
\nabla_{\beta}\left(R^{T}_{Q}\big(\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q)\big)R_{Q}\right)
and substitute (
2.65

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left|\nabla\left(R^{T}_{Q}\big(\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q)\big)R_{Q}\right)\right|^{2}\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla_{\beta}\left(R_{Q}^{T}(\partial_{t}Q+v\cdot\nabla Q+[Q,\Omega])R\right),\nabla_{\beta}\left(R^{T}_{Q}(\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q))R_{Q}\right)\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla_{\beta}\left(R^{T}_{Q}\partial_{t}QR_{Q}\right),\nabla_{\beta}\left(R^{T}_{Q}(\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q))R_{Q}\right)\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\Delta v_{i}\nabla_{j}[Q,\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)]_{ij}+\eta|\nabla\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\Big)\phi^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla^{2}Q|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2}+|\partial_{t}Q|^{2}+|\nabla Q|^{4}+|v|^{4})((|\nabla Q|^{2}+|v|^{2})\phi^{2}+|\nabla\phi|^{2})\,dx

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\nabla_{\beta}\left(R^{T}[Q,\Omega]R\right),\nabla_{\beta}\left(R^{T}_{Q}(\mathcal{H}(Q,\nabla Q)+\frac{1}{L}g_{B}(Q))R_{Q}\right)\right\rangle\phi^{2}\,dx.

(2.66)

Combining (
2.63
) with (
2.66
) and integrating in
t
, we then apply the arguments in (
2.57
)-(
